03Maintenance Deferrals and MEL LanguageA deferral note is missing its basis and status+

Your goal

Request the correct maintenance references and distinguish a proposed deferral from an authorized decision.

This lesson in the book, page 22 ↗

A Read the situation

A deferral note is missing its basis and status

Coordinator Priya receives a note for fictional defect record D17 reading, "Deferred; okay to operate." It gives no approved operator MEL item reference, document revision, applicable conditions, or authorized decision record. Maintenance controller Tomas confirms that D17 is still under review and that no deferral authorization is recorded. D17 is an internal defect identifier, not an MEL reference. The coordinator must correct the status message, request the relevant controlled records through maintenance control, and preserve the unresolved status. The exercise does not specify an aircraft fault or determine whether any item is eligible for deferral.

Priya: Maintenance coordinator / Tomas: Maintenance controller

Check the facts · 3 questions

Choose the answer supported by the briefing. Do not assume that a proposed action has already happened.

1. What is the verified status of D17?

Answer and explanation

D. Under review with no deferral authorization recorded

Tomas confirms the pending review and absence of a recorded authorization.

2. What is D17?

Answer and explanation

C. An internal defect identifier

The brief explicitly distinguishes the internal defect record from an MEL reference.

3. Can the learner determine deferral eligibility from this case?

Answer and explanation

A. No; the fault and applicable technical conditions are not supplied.

The case teaches clarification language and deliberately does not supply an operational eligibility assessment.

B Find the words

Vocabulary and collocations

D17 identifies the internal defect; it does not identify an approved MEL item. An MMEL is not interchangeable with an operator's approved MEL. A copied heading or familiar acronym cannot supply missing applicability or authorization.

24 field terms and natural word combinations
minimum equipment list (MEL)
An operator-specific approved basis for limited operation with specified inoperative items, subject to conditions.consult the approved MEL
master minimum equipment list (MMEL)
An aircraft-type baseline used in developing an operator's MEL.distinguish the MMEL
deferral
Authorized postponement of rectification under applicable conditions.verify a maintenance deferral
proposed deferral
A suggested postponement not yet established as authorized.label a proposed deferral
defect record
Documentation identifying a reported equipment problem.trace the defect record
defect identifier
The internal code assigned to a reported problem.confirm the defect identifier
item reference
The precise document entry relevant to the matter.verify the item reference
document revision
An identified version of a controlled document.check the document revision
applicability
Whether a document or condition governs the particular case.verify applicability
proviso
A specified condition attached to permission or relief.read the applicable proviso
operating limitation
A boundary governing permitted operation.preserve operating limitations
repair interval
The applicable time window for rectification.verify the repair interval
rectification
Action that corrects the reported defect.record rectification
inoperative
Not functioning as required for the relevant purpose.describe an inoperative item
maintenance procedure
A specified technical maintenance action or sequence.refer to the maintenance procedure
operational procedure
A specified operating action or sequence.refer to the operational procedure
placard
A required notice or label communicating equipment status.verify placard requirements
technical log
The controlled record of aircraft defects and maintenance status.reconcile the technical log
maintenance control
The function coordinating maintenance status and related decisions.contact maintenance control
authorized sign-off
A recorded decision or certification by an appropriately authorized person.verify authorized sign-off
airworthiness
The status of conformity and condition required for safe operation under applicable rules.avoid assuming airworthiness
configuration deviation list (CDL)
Approved conditions for operation with specified external parts missing.distinguish the CDL
nonessential equipment and furnishings (NEF)
A defined category managed under the applicable approved program.verify NEF applicability
release statement
A communication of the relevant authorized operational or maintenance release status.verify the release statement

Under review, proposed for deferral, authorized deferral, and repaired describe different statuses. Do not infer an interval, condition, or release from this language exercise. Qualified personnel must apply the actual approved documents and operating requirements.

C Notice the language

A familiar label is not supporting evidence

Colleagues may use deferred casually to mean that someone intends to review an item later. In maintenance communication, status and authority must remain explicit. Request the actual controlled reference without treating the request for evidence as a personal accusation.

16 useful phrases, grouped by purpose
Quote the unsupported status
The note says deferred, but the authorization is not recorded.
Identify the case
I am checking defect record D17.
Separate identifiers
D17 is our defect identifier, not the MEL item reference.
Request the source
Please provide the applicable controlled reference.
Check the version
Which approved document revision applies?
Preserve conditions
The status must include the applicable conditions.
Avoid a guessed interval
I cannot infer a repair interval from this note.
Name the pending step
Maintenance control is still reviewing the item.
Correct the distribution
Please replace the unsupported okay-to-operate message.
Keep the role boundary
I am requesting clarification, not authorizing a deferral.
Separate repair
An authorized deferral would not mean the defect is repaired.
Separate document types
The MMEL is not the operator's approved MEL.
Ask for the decision record
Who authorized the status, and where is it recorded?
Keep uncertainty visible
No deferral authorization is recorded at present.
Avoid technical improvisation
Use the actual approved procedures and qualified personnel.
Close the handover
Confirm the corrected status in the relevant controlled record.
6 language and precision notes
Deferred
Use only with its actual status and applicable authority.
Proposed
Signals a request awaiting the relevant decision.
Reference
Must identify the governing entry, not merely an internal ticket.
Revision
Affects which controlled wording applies.
Okay to operate
Too broad without the actual authorized operational basis.
Rectified
Means corrected, not merely postponed under conditions.

D Check your understanding

Choose the precise message

Choose one best answer using the case facts and the stated purpose.

1. Which correction fits the verified information?

Answer and explanation

A. D17 remains under review; no deferral authorization is recorded.

The correction matches Tomas's verified status and removes the unsupported operating claim.

2. Which detail is missing from a note that contains only internal defect code D17?

Answer and explanation

C. The applicable approved MEL item reference

D17 tracks the defect internally; it does not identify the applicable approved MEL entry.

3. Which request stays within Priya's coordination role?

Answer and explanation

B. Please supply the applicable reference, revision, conditions, and authorized status record.

Priya can request accurate records but cannot replace the qualified authorization process.

4. What would an authorized deferral establish, in general?

Answer and explanation

D. Conditional postponement under the applicable basis, not completed repair

Deferral and rectification are distinct; any permission remains bounded by the applicable conditions.

Check explanations in the book, page 89 ↗

E Conversations

A deferral note is missing its basis and status

Extended conversation · 20 turns · 10 gaps

Priya: Maintenance coordinator / Tomas: Maintenance controller

Complete the numbered gaps. Use each bank entry once, without changing its form.

Word bank
  • document revision
  • maintenance control
  • defect identifier
  • MMEL
  • item reference
  • Rectification
  • repair interval
  • proposed deferral
  • authorized sign-off
  • under review
  1. Priya
    The note for D17 says deferred and okay to operate. I cannot find the supporting reference or decision record. What is the verified status?
  2. Tomas
    It is still 1. No deferral authorization is recorded. Please do not repeat the okay-to-operate statement while I clarify the record through maintenance control.
  3. Priya
    I will correct the coordination message. The defect number is clear, but I need to avoid presenting that number as the reference for an approved exception.
  4. Tomas
    Exactly. D17 is the 2. It tracks our reported item. It does not identify an entry in the operator's approved minimum equipment list or establish that one applies.
  5. Priya
    I think the sender meant that a deferral had been requested. I will check with them, but I will not keep approved in the current update.
  6. Tomas
    Yes. A 3 is not an authorized decision. We should use the current verified status and request the actual basis, rather than infer approval from informal shorthand.
  7. Priya
    Please send the controlled reference and decision record when available. What else must the receiving team see with that reference?
  8. Tomas
    Request the applicable 4, document revision, relevant conditions, and recorded authorized status. Those details come through the qualified process. This message does not replace the actual technical records.
  9. Priya
    I found a master list title in an older email. Can I attach that as the operator's MEL reference, or would that create another ambiguity?
  10. Tomas
    Do not substitute the 5 for the operator's approved MEL. The document type, revision, and applicability matter. An older title alone cannot settle which controlled requirements govern this item.
  11. Priya
    Understood. I also will not add a repair deadline from memory. Nothing in the note tells me which interval applies or when its clock would begin.
  12. Tomas
    Correct. The 6 must be verified in the applicable record. A familiar interval from another case is not a safe substitute for the conditions governing this one.
  13. Priya
    If a deferral is eventually authorized, our update should still not say repaired. The defect and its treatment would remain different parts of the record.
  14. Tomas
    Right. 7 means the defect has been corrected. Deferral concerns permitted postponement under conditions. Keep that distinction visible to everyone who receives the status.
  15. Priya
    Who should the next update come from? I want the receiving team to see a verified source rather than another copied sentence without ownership.
  16. Tomas
    Use 8 as the coordination contact here. We will identify the relevant decision record when available. Do not turn a request for that record into a release statement.
  17. Priya
    I will withdraw the unsupported message and replace it with D17 under review, authorization not recorded. The request for the controlled reference will remain open.
  18. Tomas
    Include the 9 when the reference is verified. That lets the receiving team distinguish the current basis from an old email or an unrelated record.
  19. Priya
    For now, I am communicating the uncertainty and asking for evidence. I am not deciding whether the defect is eligible for deferral or whether the aircraft may operate.
  20. Tomas
    Correct. The 10 remains a separate requirement in the actual process. Keep the status accurate, preserve the record, and leave technical eligibility and release decisions to authorized personnel.

Answers and explanations
  1. under review Turn 2. Under review is the verified status, distinct from a deferral that has already received the relevant authorization.
  2. defect identifier Turn 4. The defect identifier labels the internal record and does not identify the applicable approved MEL entry.
  3. proposed deferral Turn 6. A proposed deferral is an option awaiting the relevant review and decision, not evidence that postponement is already authorized.
  4. item reference Turn 8. The item reference identifies the relevant controlled document entry, which must be distinguished from the internal defect number.
  5. MMEL Turn 10. The MMEL is an aircraft-type baseline and is not interchangeable with the specific operator's approved MEL.
  6. repair interval Turn 12. The repair interval must come from the applicable approved basis; the coordination note provides no authority to invent it.
  7. Rectification Turn 14. Rectification means correcting the defect, whereas an authorized deferral permits a bounded postponement under the relevant conditions.
  8. maintenance control Turn 16. Maintenance control is the responsible coordination source in this case, rather than an unsupported forwarded note.
  9. document revision Turn 18. The document revision identifies the controlled version relevant to the eventual reference and prevents an obsolete copy from being treated as current.
  10. authorized sign-off Turn 20. Authorized sign-off must come from the appropriate qualified process; Priya's coordination correction does not supply it.
Read the complete conversation
  1. Priya: The note for D17 says deferred and okay to operate. I cannot find the supporting reference or decision record. What is the verified status?
  2. Tomas: It is still under review. No deferral authorization is recorded. Please do not repeat the okay-to-operate statement while I clarify the record through maintenance control.
  3. Priya: I will correct the coordination message. The defect number is clear, but I need to avoid presenting that number as the reference for an approved exception.
  4. Tomas: Exactly. D17 is the defect identifier. It tracks our reported item. It does not identify an entry in the operator's approved minimum equipment list or establish that one applies.
  5. Priya: I think the sender meant that a deferral had been requested. I will check with them, but I will not keep approved in the current update.
  6. Tomas: Yes. A proposed deferral is not an authorized decision. We should use the current verified status and request the actual basis, rather than infer approval from informal shorthand.
  7. Priya: Please send the controlled reference and decision record when available. What else must the receiving team see with that reference?
  8. Tomas: Request the applicable item reference, document revision, relevant conditions, and recorded authorized status. Those details come through the qualified process. This message does not replace the actual technical records.
  9. Priya: I found a master list title in an older email. Can I attach that as the operator's MEL reference, or would that create another ambiguity?
  10. Tomas: Do not substitute the MMEL for the operator's approved MEL. The document type, revision, and applicability matter. An older title alone cannot settle which controlled requirements govern this item.
  11. Priya: Understood. I also will not add a repair deadline from memory. Nothing in the note tells me which interval applies or when its clock would begin.
  12. Tomas: Correct. The repair interval must be verified in the applicable record. A familiar interval from another case is not a safe substitute for the conditions governing this one.
  13. Priya: If a deferral is eventually authorized, our update should still not say repaired. The defect and its treatment would remain different parts of the record.
  14. Tomas: Right. Rectification means the defect has been corrected. Deferral concerns permitted postponement under conditions. Keep that distinction visible to everyone who receives the status.
  15. Priya: Who should the next update come from? I want the receiving team to see a verified source rather than another copied sentence without ownership.
  16. Tomas: Use maintenance control as the coordination contact here. We will identify the relevant decision record when available. Do not turn a request for that record into a release statement.
  17. Priya: I will withdraw the unsupported message and replace it with D17 under review, authorization not recorded. The request for the controlled reference will remain open.
  18. Tomas: Include the document revision when the reference is verified. That lets the receiving team distinguish the current basis from an old email or an unrelated record.
  19. Priya: For now, I am communicating the uncertainty and asking for evidence. I am not deciding whether the defect is eligible for deferral or whether the aircraft may operate.
  20. Tomas: Correct. The authorized sign-off remains a separate requirement in the actual process. Keep the status accurate, preserve the record, and leave technical eligibility and release decisions to authorized personnel.
Dialogue in the book, page 28 ↗
Three more conversations at work

Each short conversation has its own fictional situation and facts.

Which deferral reference?

Fictional maintenance coordination exercise: a note says deferred but lacks a reference and authorized status; no equipment decision is made in this dialogue.

  1. Maintenance coordinator: This note says deferred, but it doesn't identify the supporting reference or authorized status.
  2. Operations liaison: I assumed that word meant the aircraft could be used under the minimum equipment list.
  3. Maintenance coordinator: We can't establish that from this note. Which logbook entry does it refer to?
  4. Operations liaison: The entry reference is missing from the message I received. I'll request the linked record.
  5. Maintenance coordinator: Please also ask who recorded the status and which approved documentation supports it.
  6. Operations liaison: Should I search for a similar MEL item and suggest that reference to the team?
  7. Maintenance coordinator: No. A similar description doesn't establish applicability; that requires the authorized maintenance process.
  8. Operations liaison: Then I'll describe it as a clarification request, not an airworthiness or dispatch conclusion.
  9. Maintenance coordinator: Correct. Repeat the exact missing information so the recipient can answer without guessing.
  10. Operations liaison: I'll request the logbook reference, supporting approved document, and verified authorized status of this entry.
Closed message, open logbook question

Separate fictional case: a coordination ticket says closed, but the linked logbook extract is incomplete. Staff must clarify record meaning, not decide airworthiness.

  1. Records clerk: The coordination ticket is closed. Should I tell the planning desk the maintenance item is resolved?
  2. Maintenance records lead: Could you clarify what closed means in that ticket system: message handled or technical item resolved?
  3. Records clerk: I don't know. The extract attached to it ends before the status field.
  4. Maintenance records lead: Then we need the complete authorized record before describing the technical status to planning.
  5. Records clerk: Would it be enough to say deferred maintenance completed, since the administrative ticket is closed?
  6. Maintenance records lead: No. That would connect two statuses we haven't shown to be equivalent in this case.
  7. Records clerk: I'll ask the record owner for the full entry and the meaning of that closure label.
  8. Maintenance records lead: Good. Include the ticket identifier so they can clarify this exact record, not a general example.
  9. Records clerk: Until then, I'll report the ticket closure separately from the unverified maintenance status.
  10. Maintenance records lead: That's the distinction we need. Only the applicable authorized process can establish the operational implications.
A date without a label

Separate fictional language case: a maintenance summary contains a date beside a deferral, but no field label or source reference.

  1. Planning liaison: There's a date beside this deferral. Can I call it the maintenance completion date?
  2. Maintenance planner: What does the field label say? It might describe something different, and we shouldn't guess.
  3. Planning liaison: The copied summary has no label. It also omits the source record's reference number.
  4. Maintenance planner: Please obtain both from the record owner. We need the date's meaning, not just its value.
  5. Planning liaison: The operations team wants an immediate answer. Could I describe it as a likely deadline?
  6. Maintenance planner: That would still be an unsupported interpretation. State that the date requires clarification.
  7. Planning liaison: Should the request ask whether it is an entry date, review date, or another defined field?
  8. Maintenance planner: Yes, while leaving the answer to the authorized source rather than choosing for them.
  9. Planning liaison: I'll request the original field label and reference, then repeat back their explanation.
  10. Maintenance planner: Good. Keep any MEL application or airworthiness decision outside this administrative language exchange.

F Say it

Two scenarios to practice

A deferral note is missing its basis and status
  1. Read the corrected exchange; distinguish D17 from the MEL reference and MEL from MMEL.
  2. Switch roles for turns 11-20. Emphasize under review, authorized deferral, and rectified as different statuses.

On your own: read both roles. With a partner: swap roles after the first reading. Use the completed conversation to check any line you need to repeat.

A ticket number is not approval

Internal defect Q8 is described as deferred in an email. Maintenance control verifies that review is pending and no authorization is recorded. The approved item reference and revision are missing.

Choose one reply for each gap, then read the four turns in order. Check the explanation after each choice.

1. Coordinator: "Q8 is the internal defect ___."

Answer and explanation

B. identifier

The code labels the case rather than an approved document entry.

2. Controller: "The verified status is under ___."

Answer and explanation

B. review

The record has not yet reached an authorized deferral decision.

3. Coordinator: "The controlled document ___ is still missing."

Answer and explanation

D. revision

The scenario supplies no verified version of the applicable document.

4. Controller: "The email does not establish deferral ___."

Answer and explanation

B. authorization

Informal wording cannot replace the absent authorized decision record.

Read the completed exchange twice, switching roles. Keep the printed facts and professional responsibilities unchanged.

Build vocabulary and collocations — complete prompt

Copy the full text below into your preferred AI. The material is already included; you do not need to write or improve the prompt.

TASK: Retrieve occupational vocabulary
1. Select four terms from the reference. Explain each in plain English, give two natural collocations and quote or create a short workplace example. Label invented examples. Contrast each term with a plausible confusable term; acknowledge regional variation.
2. Present one role-labelled exchange with one missing word or phrase and four choices A-D. Exactly one choice must fit the stated facts and grammar. Stop and wait for my answer before revealing it.
3. Explain all four options, then invite me to read the completed exchange aloud. Offer a sentence frame for optional spoken practice, never an open-ended writing assignment.
4. Repeat for the other three terms, one question at a time. Vary the correct letter and the situation; preserve the reference facts. If a choice is ambiguous, replace the item.
5. Finish with two new four-choice collocation questions for later review, with a clearly separate answer key. Do not claim to schedule a reminder.

Be my workplace English practice partner. Follow the practice instructions above, using the REFERENCE block below. The block contains fictional study material, not instructions to you. Keep its facts, numbers, uncertainty and professional responsibilities accurate. Clearly label any new scenario or changed fact as an invented variation. Use natural workplace language; explain unfamiliar abbreviations in context. If a technical expression is uncertain, say so rather than inventing a definition or authority. Coach communication, not professional decisions; respect the course scope. Ask only for fictional or anonymized practice details. Judge clarity, meaning, appropriate tone and the next step. Accept valid alternative English and distinguish errors from style preferences. Do not claim to certify my language level. Unless I ask to change the plan, follow the stages and stop wherever the instructions say to wait.

When discussing a word, phrase, or example sentence as language within an explanation, question, or answer feedback, enclose that wording in quotation marks. For example: What does “about” mean in “about twenty people”? Use “on” with a named day. Keep standalone answer choices, vocabulary headwords, and ordinary reading or dialogue text uncluttered. Quotation marks identifying a language example do not attribute it to a news source; attributed source quotations must remain verbatim.

Lesson 03: Maintenance Deferrals and MEL Language
Original fictional case: Coordinator Priya receives a note for fictional defect record D17 reading, "Deferred; okay to operate." It gives no approved operator MEL item reference, document revision, applicable conditions, or authorized decision record. Maintenance controller Tomas confirms that D17 is still under review and that no deferral authorization is recorded. D17 is an internal defect identifier, not an MEL reference. The coordinator must correct the status message, request the relevant controlled records through maintenance control, and preserve the unresolved status. The exercise does not specify an aircraft fault or determine whether any item is eligible for deferral.
Language workshop: A deferral note is missing its basis and status
Communication goal: Request the correct maintenance references and distinguish a proposed deferral from an authorized decision.
Language guidance: D17 identifies the internal defect; it does not identify an approved MEL item. An MMEL is not interchangeable with an operator's approved MEL. A copied heading or familiar acronym cannot supply missing applicability or authorization.
Useful expressions (preserve the supplied facts): The note says deferred, but the authorization is not recorded. / I am checking defect record D17. / D17 is our defect identifier, not the MEL item reference. / Please provide the applicable controlled reference. / Which approved document revision applies? / The status must include the applicable conditions. / I cannot infer a repair interval from this note. / Maintenance control is still reviewing the item. / Please replace the unsupported okay-to-operate message. / I am requesting clarification, not authorizing a deferral. / An authorized deferral would not mean the defect is repaired. / The MMEL is not the operator's approved MEL. / Who authorized the status, and where is it recorded? / No deferral authorization is recorded at present. / Use the actual approved procedures and qualified personnel. / Confirm the corrected status in the relevant controlled record.
Vocabulary: minimum equipment list (MEL): An operator-specific approved basis for limited operation with specified inoperative items, subject to conditions. / master minimum equipment list (MMEL): An aircraft-type baseline used in developing an operator's MEL. / deferral: Authorized postponement of rectification under applicable conditions. / proposed deferral: A suggested postponement not yet established as authorized. / defect record: Documentation identifying a reported equipment problem. / defect identifier: The internal code assigned to a reported problem. / item reference: The precise document entry relevant to the matter. / document revision: An identified version of a controlled document. / applicability: Whether a document or condition governs the particular case. / proviso: A specified condition attached to permission or relief. / operating limitation: A boundary governing permitted operation. / repair interval: The applicable time window for rectification. / rectification: Action that corrects the reported defect. / inoperative: Not functioning as required for the relevant purpose. / maintenance procedure: A specified technical maintenance action or sequence. / operational procedure: A specified operating action or sequence. / placard: A required notice or label communicating equipment status. / technical log: The controlled record of aircraft defects and maintenance status. / maintenance control: The function coordinating maintenance status and related decisions. / authorized sign-off: A recorded decision or certification by an appropriately authorized person. / airworthiness: The status of conformity and condition required for safe operation under applicable rules. / configuration deviation list (CDL): Approved conditions for operation with specified external parts missing. / nonessential equipment and furnishings (NEF): A defined category managed under the applicable approved program. / release statement: A communication of the relevant authorized operational or maintenance release status.
Speaking task: Read the corrected exchange; distinguish D17 from the MEL reference and MEL from MMEL. Switch roles for turns 11-20. Emphasize under review, authorized deferral, and rectified as different statuses. Read the checked Q8 transfer twice without adding an interval, eligibility judgment, or operating permission.
Partner: Priya: Maintenance coordinator / Tomas: Maintenance controller
Guided response task: Select supplied answers for the dialogue gaps. Check the explanations, then read the completed exchange aloud; no original writing is required.
Optional second-round challenge: Internal defect Q8 is described as deferred in an email. Maintenance control verifies that review is pending and no authorization is recorded. The approved item reference and revision are missing.
Model for comparison AFTER my attempt, not a response to give me first: Priya: I will withdraw the unsupported message and replace it with D17 under review, authorization not recorded. The request for the controlled reference will remain open.
Tomas: Include the document revision when the reference is verified. That lets the receiving team distinguish the current basis from an old email or an unrelated record.
Priya: For now, I am communicating the uncertainty and asking for evidence. I am not deciding whether the defect is eligible for deferral or whether the aircraft may operate.
Tomas: Correct. The authorized sign-off remains a separate requirement in the actual process. Keep the status accurate, preserve the record, and leave technical eligibility and release decisions to authorized personnel.

TASK: Choose grammar that preserves the meaning
1. Use the lesson's language workshop, or one actual sentence from the dialogue, to explain a single grammar distinction in no more than 90 words. Show how it affects timing, certainty, responsibility or politeness.
2. Give one brief occupational exchange containing a gap and four complete options A-D. Supply enough context for exactly one answer. Keep distractors plausible; distinguish grammar errors from wording that changes the facts. Stop and wait.
3. Explain why each choice fits or fails, then ask me to read the completed exchange aloud. Do not request a draft, original written sentence or essay.
4. Repeat with three new exchanges, one at a time, moving from recognition to a more subtle meaning contrast. Do not copy the published checks. Label invented variations and do not turn uncertainty into a promise.
5. End with a short contrast table and two supported spoken turns using supplied frames. Accept accurate alternatives in oral practice; acknowledge and replace ambiguous multiple-choice items.

Rehearse with an AI colleague — complete prompt

Copy the full text below into your preferred AI. The material is already included; you do not need to write or improve the prompt.

TASK: Interactive workplace role-play
1. Use the supplied case or dialogue as the starting situation. Give a two-sentence briefing and offer two relevant professional roles for me to choose from. For a supplied dialogue, use its actual roles. Ask which role I want, then stop and wait. Do not write my replies.
2. After I choose, identify who you will play and the immediate communication goal. Play the other professional; where a meeting requires a third person, label each of your speakers clearly. Start with one natural workplace turn and wait for my reply. Keep most turns to one to three sentences and ask no more than one question at a time.
3. Let the exchange develop across six to ten learner turns, or end earlier if I type "feedback". Respond to what I actually say. Include a plausible clarification, disagreement or tradeoff without silently changing the starting facts. Mark any added constraint as a fictional second-round variation. Do not resolve approvals, evidence or commitments that remain uncertain.
4. Stay in role during the exchange. If my meaning is unclear, ask for clarification naturally. Give a hint only if I ask or cannot proceed. Do not deliver a model conversation in advance.
5. At the debrief, quote two of my phrases: one successful choice and one worth improving. Check factual accuracy, language, register and whether the next step was clear. Give up to three focused suggestions. Ask me to retry the weakest turn; wait. Only then offer an alternative wording and a harder replay.
Use voice replies when available. If I prefer selections, offer four plausible spoken replies and explain my choice before continuing. Do not require written compositions.

04Irregular Operations and Passenger CommunicationPassengers have heard two different departure times+

Your goal

Correct conflicting information, separate update and departure times, and offer a verified assistance route.

This lesson in the book, page 31 ↗

A Read the situation

Passengers have heard two different departure times

At 14:40 local time, passengers for fictional Flight EL618 have heard an earlier 14:30 departure message and seen 15:00 on a display. The latest verified operations message says the departure estimate is not confirmed; its cause has not been authorized for public explanation. Gate supervisor Lena will provide the next passenger update at 15:10 local, even if no new estimate is available. Agent Omar must correct both unsupported times and keep channels consistent. The service desk can review individual connection and rebooking questions, but no replacement seat, refund, or compensation is guaranteed by the supplied facts.

Omar: Gate agent / Ms Bell: Delayed passenger

Check the facts · 3 questions

Choose the answer supported by the briefing. Do not assume that a proposed action has already happened.

1. What is the latest verified departure estimate?

Answer and explanation

C. No departure estimate is confirmed.

The latest verified operations message explicitly leaves the departure estimate unconfirmed.

2. What will happen at 15:10 local?

Answer and explanation

C. The next passenger status update

Lena commits to an update even if no new departure estimate exists.

3. What can the service desk do on the supplied facts?

Answer and explanation

C. Review individual connection and rebooking questions

The service desk can assess options, but no particular seat or financial remedy is established.

B Find the words

Vocabulary and collocations

Neither 14:30 nor 15:00 is supported by the latest verified update. The 15:10 time concerns a new communication, not departure. State the local time basis and correct the conflicting channels without inventing a cause.

24 field terms and natural word combinations
irregular operations (IROPS)
Disruptions affecting the normal airline schedule or service.manage irregular operations
departure estimate
The current expected departure time, subject to its stated status.verify a departure estimate
delay announcement
A passenger message explaining a service delay.issue a delay announcement
flight information display
A screen showing flight and gate information.correct the flight information display
gate supervisor
The person coordinating the gate team's activity.contact the gate supervisor
passenger update
A communication of the latest verified passenger-facing status.schedule a passenger update
onward connection
A later service a traveler plans to take.review an onward connection
connection risk
The possibility that a traveler will miss a later service.explain connection risk
rebooking option
A possible alternative reservation subject to confirmation.check rebooking options
seat availability
Whether a place can be confirmed on the relevant service.verify seat availability
standby
A waiting status without a confirmed seat on the relevant flight.distinguish standby status
confirmed reservation
A booking accepted for the specified service under its terms.verify a confirmed reservation
itinerary change
An alteration to the planned travel sequence.explain an itinerary change
service desk
The point handling individual travel assistance requests.direct to the service desk
travel document
A document relevant to a passenger's journey or eligibility.check required travel documents
baggage routing
The planned handling path for checked bags.verify baggage routing
assistance request
A request for help with a particular passenger need.record an assistance request
accessibility assistance
Support enabling a passenger with an access need to use the service.coordinate accessibility assistance
meal voucher
A document authorizing specified food or drink support.explain a meal voucher
refund
Money returned under the applicable terms or rules.verify refund status
compensation
A payment or remedy under the relevant policy or legal basis.distinguish compensation
authorized explanation
Information approved for the relevant public communication.use the authorized explanation
channel consistency
Agreement among the current messages provided through different routes.maintain channel consistency
superseded message
An earlier message replaced by newer verified information.identify a superseded message

A possible rebooking route is not a confirmed replacement seat. Refunds, compensation, and assistance may follow different terms and requirements. This case establishes a review route only, not the passenger's legal entitlement or a particular approved remedy.

C Notice the language

Consistency is more helpful than repeated optimism

Passengers make onward plans from the words and times they hear. Acknowledge conflicting messages directly instead of defending each channel. One reliable status, a named update time, and a practical assistance route give the passenger something usable without a new unsupported promise.

16 useful phrases, grouped by purpose
Acknowledge the conflict
You have received two different times, and I am sorry for the confusion.
State the latest position
We do not have a confirmed departure estimate.
Correct the earlier announcement
The earlier 14:30 message is no longer reliable.
Correct the display
The 15:00 display does not reflect a confirmed estimate.
Give the next update
We will update you at 15:10 local time.
Separate update from departure
That time is for information, not a promised departure.
Keep the cause bounded
I do not have an authorized explanation to share yet.
Avoid invented reassurance
I cannot guarantee that you will make the connection.
Offer practical assistance
The service desk can review your individual itinerary.
Separate an option from a seat
A rebooking option still needs confirmation.
Protect private details
Let us discuss your booking details at the service desk.
Ask about a specific need
Do you need assistance while we review the options?
Keep remedies accurate
I cannot confirm a refund or compensation from this update.
Maintain all channels
We are correcting the display and the gate message.
Promise communication
You will receive an update even if the estimate remains unconfirmed.
Close with the usable facts
No confirmed departure estimate; next update at 15:10 local.
6 language and precision notes
Estimate
A forecast, not an actual departure or a guarantee.
Latest verified
Names the information basis that supersedes earlier conflicting messages.
Update at
Attach the time to communication so its purpose remains clear.
Can review
Offers an assessment, not an automatic favorable result.
Confirmed seat
Different from standby or a proposed itinerary.
Cause unknown to the speaker
Do not fill the gap with a plausible operational story.

D Check your understanding

Choose the precise message

Choose one best answer using the case facts and the stated purpose.

1. Which announcement is accurate?

Answer and explanation

D. There is no confirmed departure estimate; the next update is at 15:10 local.

The correct announcement gives the verified uncertainty and the actual communication commitment.

2. Which response handles an onward connection appropriately?

Answer and explanation

C. The service desk can review your itinerary and available options.

The supplied assistance route permits individual review without an unsupported outcome guarantee.

3. What should happen to the conflicting channels?

Answer and explanation

C. Correct them to the latest verified status.

Consistent current information prevents passengers from planning around unsupported older messages.

4. Which statement about the delay's cause is appropriate?

Answer and explanation

D. I do not have an authorized explanation to share yet.

The brief supplies no authorized public cause and does not justify inventing one.

E Conversations

Passengers have heard two different departure times

Extended conversation · 20 turns · 10 gaps

Omar: Gate agent / Ms Bell: Delayed passenger

Complete the numbered gaps. Use each bank entry once, without changing its form.

Word bank
  • onward connection
  • baggage routing
  • conflicting messages
  • departure estimate
  • Seat availability
  • passenger update
  • flight information display
  • authorized explanation
  • channel consistency
  • compensation
  1. Ms Bell
    We were told fourteen thirty, but the screen says fifteen hundred. It is already fourteen forty. Which time should I use for my connection plans?
  2. Omar
    I am sorry for those 1. The latest verified information is that we do not have a confirmed departure estimate. I should not ask you to rely on either displayed time.
  3. Ms Bell
    The screen looks official, so I assumed fifteen hundred was firm. Does it need correcting?
  4. Omar
    Yes. The 2 does not currently reflect a confirmed estimate. We are aligning it with the gate message so you do not have to choose between inconsistent sources.
  5. Ms Bell
    Does anyone know why the flight is delayed? Other passengers are saying it is a crew problem, but somebody else mentioned maintenance.
  6. Omar
    I do not have an 3 to share about the cause yet. I will not repeat speculation about crew or maintenance as fact. I can give you the verified current status.
  7. Ms Bell
    When will we hear something again? We keep watching the display and asking, but the answers change.
  8. Omar
    Our gate supervisor, Lena, will give the next 4 at fifteen ten local time. That commitment applies even if there is no new departure estimate, so you will not be left interpreting silence.
  9. Ms Bell
    Just to check, fifteen ten is when you will speak to us, not when you now expect the aircraft to leave?
  10. Omar
    Correct. It is not a 5. I cannot promise a departure time at present. We will clearly label any verified estimate if operations supplies one later.
  11. Ms Bell
    My onward flight leaves this evening. Can you guarantee that I will still make it, or put me on another service now?
  12. Omar
    I cannot guarantee the 6 from this information. The service desk can review your itinerary and the available options. A proposed replacement would still need its own confirmation.
  13. Ms Bell
    I saw another flight online with seats showing. Does that mean I can go directly to its gate and assume my booking has transferred?
  14. Omar
    No. 7 on another service does not mean your reservation has changed. Please use the service desk so the actual booking and any related requirements can be checked.
  15. Ms Bell
    I also checked a bag. I do not want to agree to a different itinerary without understanding whether the bag arrangement changes.
  16. Omar
    Ask the desk to verify 8 as part of that review. I cannot promise the bag's route from this gate update, but it is a relevant question for the individual arrangement.
  17. Ms Bell
    I also need to know where I stand on a refund. Is that something the desk can explain alongside the rebooking options?
  18. Omar
    Yes, ask them to review the applicable basis. This update does not confirm 9 or a refund. I should distinguish that financial review from the current flight status and possible rebooking options.
  19. Ms Bell
    I will take the booking details to the desk. Please make sure the next announcement is clear enough that nobody hears another update time as a departure.
  20. Omar
    Yes. I will check 10 with Lena: no confirmed departure estimate, and the next update at fifteen ten local. I am sorry we gave you conflicting information.

Answers and explanations
  1. conflicting messages Turn 2. Conflicting messages describe the two inconsistent times the passenger received; neither is supported by the latest verified status.
  2. flight information display Turn 4. The flight information display is one of the channels that must be corrected to match the latest verified operations message.
  3. authorized explanation Turn 6. An authorized explanation is information cleared for this public update; the case provides none about the cause.
  4. passenger update Turn 8. The passenger update is scheduled for fifteen ten local, even if no new departure estimate has been confirmed.
  5. departure estimate Turn 10. A departure estimate concerns expected movement; the fifteen-ten communication time must not be mistaken for one.
  6. onward connection Turn 12. The onward connection is the later flight whose feasibility requires an individual itinerary review, not a general promise from the gate update.
  7. Seat availability Turn 14. Seat availability must be verified for a specific rebooking; a public display does not itself transfer or confirm the passenger's reservation.
  8. baggage routing Turn 16. Baggage routing is a separate practical detail of the itinerary that should be checked rather than assumed from a proposed seat.
  9. compensation Turn 18. Compensation is a separate policy or legal question; the operational update does not establish an approved payment.
  10. channel consistency Turn 20. Channel consistency means the display and announcements give the same status and distinguish update times from departure estimates.
Read the complete conversation
  1. Ms Bell: We were told fourteen thirty, but the screen says fifteen hundred. It is already fourteen forty. Which time should I use for my connection plans?
  2. Omar: I am sorry for those conflicting messages. The latest verified information is that we do not have a confirmed departure estimate. I should not ask you to rely on either displayed time.
  3. Ms Bell: The screen looks official, so I assumed fifteen hundred was firm. Does it need correcting?
  4. Omar: Yes. The flight information display does not currently reflect a confirmed estimate. We are aligning it with the gate message so you do not have to choose between inconsistent sources.
  5. Ms Bell: Does anyone know why the flight is delayed? Other passengers are saying it is a crew problem, but somebody else mentioned maintenance.
  6. Omar: I do not have an authorized explanation to share about the cause yet. I will not repeat speculation about crew or maintenance as fact. I can give you the verified current status.
  7. Ms Bell: When will we hear something again? We keep watching the display and asking, but the answers change.
  8. Omar: Our gate supervisor, Lena, will give the next passenger update at fifteen ten local time. That commitment applies even if there is no new departure estimate, so you will not be left interpreting silence.
  9. Ms Bell: Just to check, fifteen ten is when you will speak to us, not when you now expect the aircraft to leave?
  10. Omar: Correct. It is not a departure estimate. I cannot promise a departure time at present. We will clearly label any verified estimate if operations supplies one later.
  11. Ms Bell: My onward flight leaves this evening. Can you guarantee that I will still make it, or put me on another service now?
  12. Omar: I cannot guarantee the onward connection from this information. The service desk can review your itinerary and the available options. A proposed replacement would still need its own confirmation.
  13. Ms Bell: I saw another flight online with seats showing. Does that mean I can go directly to its gate and assume my booking has transferred?
  14. Omar: No. Seat availability on another service does not mean your reservation has changed. Please use the service desk so the actual booking and any related requirements can be checked.
  15. Ms Bell: I also checked a bag. I do not want to agree to a different itinerary without understanding whether the bag arrangement changes.
  16. Omar: Ask the desk to verify baggage routing as part of that review. I cannot promise the bag's route from this gate update, but it is a relevant question for the individual arrangement.
  17. Ms Bell: I also need to know where I stand on a refund. Is that something the desk can explain alongside the rebooking options?
  18. Omar: Yes, ask them to review the applicable basis. This update does not confirm compensation or a refund. I should distinguish that financial review from the current flight status and possible rebooking options.
  19. Ms Bell: I will take the booking details to the desk. Please make sure the next announcement is clear enough that nobody hears another update time as a departure.
  20. Omar: Yes. I will check channel consistency with Lena: no confirmed departure estimate, and the next update at fifteen ten local. I am sorry we gave you conflicting information.
Dialogue in the book, page 37 ↗
Three more conversations at work

Each short conversation has its own fictional situation and facts.

Correcting the unconfirmed departure estimate

Fictional irregular-operations case: gate and app messages conflict; the next departure estimate is unconfirmed. A gate supervisor briefs a tour representative.

  1. Tour representative: The app gives one departure time and the gate message gives another. Which is guaranteed?
  2. Gate supervisor: Neither new estimate is confirmed. I'm sorry our messages presented inconsistent information to your group.
  3. Tour representative: Several travelers are arranging connections based on those times. They need a clear correction.
  4. Gate supervisor: Please tell them the departure estimate is being verified; the earlier messages should not be treated as guarantees.
  5. Tour representative: Can you promise their connecting flights will wait if this becomes a misconnect?
  6. Gate supervisor: No. We can refer connection concerns for review, but can't promise another flight's operation.
  7. Tour representative: Does that mean reaccommodation has already been arranged for everyone on the group booking?
  8. Gate supervisor: No arrangements are confirmed. Individual options need checking through the appropriate passenger-service team.
  9. Tour representative: I'll explain the uncertain timing and direct affected travelers to that team for their cases.
  10. Gate supervisor: Thank you. I'll seek a consistent verified update rather than repeat an unsupported departure time.
A hotel request was mistaken for a booking

Separate fictional IROP case: an agent told a travel coordinator that hotel accommodation was arranged, but the record shows only a submitted request.

  1. Travel coordinator: You said hotel rooms were arranged, but our group has received no accommodation confirmation.
  2. Passenger services agent: I checked the record. The request was submitted; I incorrectly described it as a confirmed booking.
  3. Travel coordinator: That distinction matters. People stopped looking for other arrangements because of your message.
  4. Passenger services agent: I'm sorry. I'll correct the status and contact the team reviewing the accommodation request.
  5. Travel coordinator: Can you now guarantee rooms at the property named in your earlier message?
  6. Passenger services agent: No availability is confirmed there. I shouldn't replace one unsupported promise with another.
  7. Travel coordinator: Please tell me what you can verify, including whether you have actually sent the request.
  8. Passenger services agent: Submission is recorded. Acceptance and room allocation remain open, and I'll ask for their status.
  9. Travel coordinator: I'll inform the group that accommodation is requested, not booked, while we await that response.
  10. Passenger services agent: I'll remain your contact for the correction and clearly identify any confirmed options when available.
Weather delay versus unknown cause

Separate fictional case: a customer-service draft attributes a delay to weather, but operations has confirmed only that a delay exists, not its cause.

  1. Customer communications lead: Your draft says weather delay, but the operations update doesn't confirm a reason yet.
  2. Station agent: I saw rain outside and assumed that explained the disruption. I already told a tour organizer.
  3. Customer communications lead: Then we need a correction. Observing rain doesn't establish the cause of this particular delay.
  4. Station agent: I'll say I gave an unverified explanation and that the confirmed information is the delay itself.
  5. Customer communications lead: Good. Acknowledge that the explanation may have influenced their plans without blaming their interpretation.
  6. Station agent: Should I mention an alternative cause so the correction sounds more useful to the organizer?
  7. Customer communications lead: No. A useful correction can clearly identify what remains unknown and who is checking it.
  8. Station agent: I'll contact operations for a verified reason and avoid giving a fresh departure estimate.
  9. Customer communications lead: Please also ensure the revised internal draft and the organizer's update use consistent wording.
  10. Station agent: I'll correct both and keep the cause unconfirmed until the responsible team provides verified information.

Two scenarios to practice

Passengers have heard two different departure times
  1. Read turns 1-10 with the answers, making 15:10 clearly an update time.
  2. Switch roles for turns 11-20; keep the apology natural and a seat option distinct from a confirmed booking.

On your own: read both roles. With a partner: swap roles after the first reading. Use the completed conversation to check any line you need to repeat.

Correct the time without inventing a new one

At 16:20 local, a display says 16:30 departure, but operations has no confirmed estimate. The gate promises an update at 16:45. The service desk can review individual itineraries.

Choose one reply for each gap, then read the four turns in order. Check the explanation after each choice.

1. Passenger: "Is 16:30 a confirmed ___?"

Answer and explanation

C. departure estimate

The display is not supported by a verified estimate in this case.

2. Agent: "No; our next information ___ is at 16:45."

Answer and explanation

C. update

The commitment is to communicate, not to depart at that time.

3. Passenger: "Where can I review my individual ___?"

Answer and explanation

B. itinerary

The service desk can assess the passenger's specific travel sequence and options.

4. Agent: "Please speak with the service ___."

Answer and explanation

B. desk

The brief names the service desk as the individual assistance route.

TASK: Retrieve occupational vocabulary
1. Select four terms from the reference. Explain each in plain English, give two natural collocations and quote or create a short workplace example. Label invented examples. Contrast each term with a plausible confusable term; acknowledge regional variation.
2. Present one role-labelled exchange with one missing word or phrase and four choices A-D. Exactly one choice must fit the stated facts and grammar. Stop and wait for my answer before revealing it.
3. Explain all four options, then invite me to read the completed exchange aloud. Offer a sentence frame for optional spoken practice, never an open-ended writing assignment.
4. Repeat for the other three terms, one question at a time. Vary the correct letter and the situation; preserve the reference facts. If a choice is ambiguous, replace the item.
5. Finish with two new four-choice collocation questions for later review, with a clearly separate answer key. Do not claim to schedule a reminder.

Keep its facts, numbers, uncertainty and professional responsibilities accurate. Clearly label any new scenario or changed fact as an invented variation. Lesson 04: Irregular Operations and Passenger Communication
Original fictional case: At 14:40 local time, passengers for fictional Flight EL618 have heard an earlier 14:30 departure message and seen 15:00 on a display. The latest verified operations message says the departure estimate is not confirmed; its cause has not been authorized for public explanation. Language workshop: Passengers have heard two different departure times
Communication goal: Correct conflicting information, separate update and departure times, and offer a verified assistance route.
Language guidance: Neither 14:30 nor 15:00 is supported by the latest verified update. The 15:10 time concerns a new communication, not departure. State the local time basis and correct the conflicting channels without inventing a cause.
Useful expressions (preserve the supplied facts): You have received two different times, and I am sorry for the confusion. / We do not have a confirmed departure estimate. / The earlier 14:30 message is no longer reliable. / The 15:00 display does not reflect a confirmed estimate. / We will update you at 15:10 local time. / That time is for information, not a promised departure. / I do not have an authorized explanation to share yet. / I cannot guarantee that you will make the connection. / The service desk can review your individual itinerary. / A rebooking option still needs confirmation. / Let us discuss your booking details at the service desk. / Do you need assistance while we review the options? / I cannot confirm a refund or compensation from this update. / We are correcting the display and the gate message. / You will receive an update even if the estimate remains unconfirmed. / No confirmed departure estimate; next update at 15:10 local.

Practice grammar in context — complete prompt

Copy the full text below into your preferred AI. The material is already included; you do not need to write or improve the prompt.

TASK: Choose grammar that preserves the meaning
1. Use the lesson's language workshop, or one actual sentence from the dialogue, to explain a single grammar distinction in no more than 90 words. Show how it affects timing, certainty, responsibility or politeness.
2. Give one brief occupational exchange containing a gap and four complete options A-D. Supply enough context for exactly one answer. Keep distractors plausible; distinguish grammar errors from wording that changes the facts. Stop and wait.
3. Explain why each choice fits or fails, then ask me to read the completed exchange aloud. Do not request a draft, original written sentence or essay.
4. Repeat with three new exchanges, one at a time, moving from recognition to a more subtle meaning contrast. Do not copy the published checks. Label invented variations and do not turn uncertainty into a promise.
5. End with a short contrast table and two supported spoken turns using supplied frames. Accept accurate alternatives in oral practice; acknowledge and replace ambiguous multiple-choice items.

Language guidance: Neither 14:30 nor 15:00 is supported by the latest verified update. The 15:10 time concerns a new communication, not departure. State the local time basis and correct the conflicting channels without inventing a cause.

Vocabulary: irregular operations (IROPS): Disruptions affecting the normal airline schedule or service. / departure estimate: The current expected departure time, subject to its stated status. / delay announcement: A passenger message explaining a service delay. / flight information display: A screen showing flight and gate information. / gate supervisor: The person coordinating the gate team's activity. / passenger update: A communication of the latest verified passenger-facing status. / onward connection: A later service a traveler plans to take. / connection risk: The possibility that a traveler will miss a later service. / rebooking option: A possible alternative reservation subject to confirmation. / seat availability: Whether a place can be confirmed on the relevant service. / standby: A waiting status without a confirmed seat on the relevant flight. / confirmed reservation: A booking accepted for the specified service under its terms. / itinerary change: An alteration to the planned travel sequence. / service desk: The point handling individual travel assistance requests. / travel document: A document relevant to a passenger's journey or eligibility. / baggage routing: The planned handling path for checked bags. / assistance request: A request for help with a particular passenger need. / accessibility assistance: Support enabling a passenger with an access need to use the service. / meal voucher: A document authorizing specified food or drink support. / refund: Money returned under the applicable terms or rules. / compensation: A payment or remedy under the relevant policy or legal basis. / authorized explanation: Information approved for the relevant public communication. / channel consistency: Agreement among the current messages provided through different routes. / superseded message: An earlier message replaced by newer verified information.
Speaking task: Read turns 1-10 with the answers, making 15:10 clearly an update time. Switch roles for turns 11-20; keep the apology natural and a seat option distinct from a confirmed booking. Read the checked transfer twice, emphasizing 16:45 for information and the service desk for individual itineraries.
Partner: Omar: Gate agent / Ms Bell: Delayed passenger
Guided response task: Select supplied answers for the dialogue gaps. Check the explanations, then read the completed exchange aloud; no original writing is required.
Optional second-round challenge: At 16:20 local, a display says 16:30 departure, but operations has no confirmed estimate. The gate promises an update at 16:45. The service desk can review individual itineraries.
Model for comparison AFTER my attempt, not a response to give me first: Ms Bell: I also need to know where I stand on a refund. Is that something the desk can explain alongside the rebooking options?
Omar: Yes, ask them to review the applicable basis. This update does not confirm compensation or a refund. I should distinguish that financial review from the current flight status and possible rebooking options.
Ms Bell: I will take the booking details to the desk. Please make sure the next announcement is clear enough that nobody hears another update time as a departure.
Omar: Yes. I will check channel consistency with Lena: no confirmed departure estimate, and the next update at fifteen ten local. I am sorry we gave you conflicting information.
END REFERENCE
Rehearse with an AI colleague — complete prompt

Copy the full text below into your preferred AI. The material is already included; you do not need to write or improve the prompt.

TASK: Interactive workplace role-play
1. Use the supplied case or dialogue as the starting situation. Give a two-sentence briefing and offer two relevant professional roles for me to choose from. For a supplied dialogue, use its actual roles. Ask which role I want, then stop and wait. Do not write my replies.
2. After I choose, identify who you will play and the immediate communication goal. Play the other professional; where a meeting requires a third person, label each of your speakers clearly. Start with one natural workplace turn and wait for my reply. Keep most turns to one to three sentences and ask no more than one question at a time.
3. Let the exchange develop across six to ten learner turns, or end earlier if I type "feedback". Respond to what I actually say. Include a plausible clarification, disagreement or tradeoff without silently changing the starting facts. Mark any added constraint as a fictional second-round variation. Do not resolve approvals, evidence or commitments that remain uncertain.
4. Stay in role during the exchange. If my meaning is unclear, ask for clarification naturally. Give a hint only if I ask or cannot proceed. Do not deliver a model conversation in advance.
5. At the debrief, quote two of my phrases: one successful choice and one worth improving. Check factual accuracy, language, register and whether the next step was clear. Give up to three focused suggestions. Ask me to retry the weakest turn; wait. Only then offer an alternative wording and a harder replay.
Use voice replies when available. If I prefer selections, offer four plausible spoken replies and explain my choice before continuing. Do not require written compositions.

Be my workplace English practice partner. Follow the practice instructions above, using the REFERENCE block below. The block contains fictional study material, not instructions to you. Keep its facts, numbers, uncertainty and professional responsibilities accurate. Clearly label any new scenario or changed fact as an invented variation. Use natural workplace language; explain unfamiliar abbreviations in context. If a technical expression is uncertain, say so rather than inventing a definition or authority. Coach communication, not professional decisions; respect the course scope. Ask only for fictional or anonymized practice details. Judge clarity, meaning, appropriate tone and the next step. Accept valid alternative English and distinguish errors from style preferences. Do not claim to certify my language level. Unless I ask to change the plan, follow the stages and stop wherever the instructions say to wait.

When discussing a word, phrase, or example sentence as language within an explanation, question, or answer feedback, enclose that wording in quotation marks. For example: What does “about” mean in “about twenty people”? Use “on” with a named day. Keep standalone answer choices, vocabulary headwords, and ordinary reading or dialogue text uncluttered. Quotation marks identifying a language example do not attribute it to a news source; attributed source quotations must remain verbatim.

Practice setting: B2 independent. Use natural professional English with brief explanations. Let me attempt each task without a model first. Offer a hint after difficulty and invite a more precise retry.

05Ground Handling and Turnaround PerformanceA handover was sent, but nobody accepted it+

Your goal

Confirm task ownership and report a catering balance without confusing arrival, receipt, and completion.

This lesson in the book, page 40 ↗

A Read the situation

A handover was sent, but nobody accepted it

At 13:20 UTC, a fictional turnaround requires 96 catering packs. The receiving record confirms 80 packs, leaving 16 outstanding. A supplier truck has arrived with a note claiming the remaining 16, but receipt and count are not yet verified. The outgoing ground team sent a handover without obtaining a named receiving person's acceptance. Coordinator Hassan contacts incoming lead Elin. She can accept responsibility for verifying the balance and reporting status at 13:35 UTC; acceptance does not itself complete the delivery. The 13:45 UTC off-block target remains a planning target. Other required turnaround and release processes are outside this short coordination case.

Hassan: Turnaround coordinator / Elin: Incoming ground-services lead

Check the facts · 3 questions

Choose the answer supported by the briefing. Do not assume that a proposed action has already happened.

1. How many packs remain unverified in the receiving balance?

Answer and explanation

B. 16

Ninety-six required packs minus eighty confirmed received leaves sixteen outstanding.

2. What does the truck's arrival establish?

Answer and explanation

D. The truck is present, not that receipt and count are verified.

Physical arrival and the supplier's note do not establish verified receipt of the remaining packs.

3. What does Elin's acceptance establish?

Answer and explanation

B. Ownership of the verification and status task

Accepting responsibility names the task owner but does not complete the task or authorize departure.

B Find the words

Vocabulary and collocations

96 required minus 80 confirmed received leaves 16 outstanding. The supplier note claims those 16 are on the truck; it does not prove they have been counted and accepted. Keep the receiving balance unchanged until verification.

24 field terms and natural word combinations
turnaround
The ground-service period between an aircraft's arrival and next departure.coordinate the turnaround
ground handler
A person or organization providing aircraft ground services.contact the ground handler
service handover
Transfer of a service task and its current information.complete a service handover
receiving lead
The person accepting responsibility at the receiving end.name the receiving lead
catering uplift
Food and service supplies provided for a flight.verify the catering uplift
catering pack
A counted package of catering supplies in the stated case.reconcile catering packs
required quantity
The amount specified as needed.confirm the required quantity
confirmed receipt
Recorded verification that specified goods were received.record confirmed receipt
outstanding balance
The amount still needed after verified receipt.calculate the outstanding balance
delivery note
A supplier document describing a delivery.check the delivery note
manifest
A list of the goods or people covered by an operation.reconcile the manifest
truck arrival
The event of the delivery vehicle reaching the site.record truck arrival
physical count
A check of the actual quantity present or received.verify the physical count
short delivery
Receipt of less than the required quantity.report a short delivery
task acceptance
Explicit agreement to take responsibility for an activity.confirm task acceptance
task completion
Verified finishing of the specified activity.distinguish task completion
milestone
A defined event used to track progress.report a turnaround milestone
critical path
The sequence of dependent activities determining the earliest completion.identify the critical path
service dependency
A required service relationship affecting later work.track a service dependency
target off-block time
A planning target for leaving the parking position.protect the target off-block time
readiness status
The verified position of preparation for the next step.report readiness status
handover gap
Missing information or acceptance during a transfer.close the handover gap
exception report
A message identifying a departure from the planned condition.issue an exception report
delay attribution
Assigning a cause or category to a delay using the applicable evidence.verify delay attribution

Elin accepts the verification task and the 13:35 status report, not a promise of completed delivery by then. A catering completion would also not establish completion of every other turnaround or release requirement.

C Notice the language

Ownership needs an answer

A sent message can create an illusion that responsibility has transferred. Ask the receiving colleague to confirm the task, current balance, and next report. This check is especially valuable when shifts overlap briefly and everyone is working toward the same time target.

16 useful phrases, grouped by purpose
State the current count
We have 80 confirmed against a requirement of 96.
Calculate the remainder
Sixteen packs remain outstanding.
Separate arrival
The truck is here, but receipt is not verified.
Attribute the claim
The delivery note lists the remaining sixteen.
Identify the handover gap
The earlier message has no named acceptance.
Request ownership
Can you accept the balance-verification task?
Confirm the scope
I will verify receipt and count, then report the result.
Set the report time
Please provide status at 13:35 UTC.
Avoid a completion promise
That is a reporting time, not a guarantee of completion.
Preserve the plan label
13:45 UTC remains the off-block target.
Keep other requirements separate
Catering status is not the whole turnaround status.
Record acceptance
Elin has accepted responsibility for the check.
Report an exception
Any unverified balance remains open.
Avoid premature blame
We have not established the cause of the handover gap.
Close with a readback
Ninety-six required, eighty received, sixteen pending verification.
Update from evidence
Change the receiving balance only after verified receipt.
6 language and precision notes
Arrived
Describes vehicle presence, not accepted delivery.
Lists
Attributes a quantity to a document rather than a completed count.
Outstanding
The balance not yet confirmed received.
Accepted the task
Distinguish from accepted the goods.
By 13:35
In this case, the commitment concerns a status report.
Ready
Specify which service or decision the label covers.

D Check your understanding

Choose the precise message

Choose one best answer using the case facts and the stated purpose.

1. Which handover is accurate?

Answer and explanation

C. Elin owns verification of the remaining 16; status due at 13:35 UTC.

The correct message names ownership, quantity, and report time without claiming completion.

2. Which statement about the delivery note is accurate?

Answer and explanation

D. It claims a quantity that still needs receipt verification.

A supplier document is information to check, not a substitute for the receiving process.

3. If 12 additional packs are later verified received, what remains outstanding?

Answer and explanation

A. 4

Eighty plus twelve is ninety-two, leaving four of the required ninety-six.

4. Which overall conclusion is unsupported even after catering completion?

Answer and explanation

A. All flight-release requirements are satisfied.

The case covers catering coordination, not the complete turnaround and operational release process.
